Technical lineage and meaningful facts consumed

I read the change-control reports, retained correction chronology, policy/result registers,
and architecture specification. The central story is governed strategy correction and
source-preserving lifecycle reconstruction, not a new merchant population ora
production-performance claim.

The architecture’s numerical baseline remains 750 synthetic merchants, two alternative
scenarios, 1,500 application-scenario paths, 6,519 reportable structures, 130 funded
scenario paths, and 15,600 monitoring account-days. The funded split is 103 Baseline
and 27 Recession Energy. These grains cannot be interchanged or relabeled as distinct real
customers.

Three distinctions will be particularly important when production begins:

Module 1 needs substantive lineage, not merely stage names. There are exactly 13 core
stages M1.2-M1.14, followed by three distinct stages. M1.15 owns the application
consumption contract; M1.16 owns acquisition-source, attribution, and cost
companion evidence; M1.17 owns end-to-end QA and G2 acceptance. G0/G1 sit above
that chain as the control plane.

Counter-offer construction and authority are different boundaries. M2.2
constructs/tests permissible structures; M2.3 owns final authorization or review. A
supportable alternative on a review path is not funded approval. The 40% minimum
amount, 60% maximum reduction, 30/60/90-day horizons, economic hurdle, authority-
first selection, and stress floor must remain intact.

Lifecycle measures must retain stage ownership. The exposure bridge is M2.5 attention
exposure $7,823.28, less $1,762.25 processed, yielding M2.10 certified exposure

### 0041 · assistant · timestamp unknown
**Record:** `P4-P035-S03` · **Fidelity:** `OCR_DERIVED_NORMALIZED_TRANSCRIPTION_PAGE_IMAGE_REVIEWED`
**Source:** P4 pages 35 · **Literal time label:** none
**Editorial/source-status annotations:** Duplicate rendered/LaTeX formula normalized from the visible page image.

<!-- BEGIN_RECORD P4-P035-S03 d6f0435e2019ac45e0ed45003c666af29f78e71ccab204edfa4356f807c8d287 -->
This is illustrating the remaining-balance cap in the simplified remittance example on
Page 2.

Suppose the illustrative terms are:

e 10% of eligible sales is the remittance share.
e Today's eligible sales would ordinarily produce a $200 remittance — e.g., $2,000 of
sales x 10% = $200.

e But only $80 remains on the repayment balance.

You would not collect the full $200, because that would exceed the remaining amount due.
The simplified formula is:

Remittance = min(10% of eligible sales, remaining balance)

So:

min($200, $80) = $80

[The duplicate rendered/LaTeX expression is normalized from the visible page, not treated as a second calculation.]

After that $80 remittance, the illustrative remaining balance becomes $0.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P035-S03 -->

### 0042 · assistant · timestamp unknown
**Record:** `P4-P036-S01` · **Fidelity:** `OCR_DERIVED_NORMALIZED_TRANSCRIPTION_PAGE_IMAGE_REVIEWED`
**Source:** P4 pages 36 · **Literal time label:** none

<!-- BEGIN_RECORD P4-P036-S01 bd3979e0866b730498d1aba3cf7340b661d1678a2f859eadd1df71dcca81473b -->
Why I put it in the teaching deck

It teaches an important distinction: the sales-linked percentage determines the
provisional remittance, but the outstanding balance caps what can actually be
collected in this simplified example.

So if the normal calculation says $200 but only $80 remains, you collect $80—not $200
and certainly not a negative remaining balance.

The underlying learning guide describes exactly this simplified rule and explicitly notes that
itis illustrative arithmetic, not the R26 remittance generator or a real contract.

One thing | would improve

Looking at the callout by itself, “$80 remains” is too context-dependent. A learner could
reasonably ask, “$80 of what remains?”

I would make it:

THE BALANCE CAP
Only $80 remains to repay

The sales-share calculation produces $200, but the remaining balance caps this
illustrative remittance at $80.

That is materially clearer and more pedagogically complete.

22-page review PDF — before the final editorial corrections
What is materially deeper

Merchant evidence now leads to an actual capacity calculation. The deck works
through merchant 098’s eligible sales, the source’s industry-margin assumption, existing
obligations, proposed repayment burden, residual operating cash, and projected liquidity.
It distinguishes observed balances from modeled buffers and shows why strong sales
alone do not establish capacity.

Request terms now interact mathematically. A controlled sensitivity lesson changes
amount, remittance rate, and horizon separately. It shows when the sales-share
calculation binds and when the horizon-based repayment demand binds—and why
reducing one parameter does not always reduce assessed burden. These variations are
expressly labeled illustrative, rather than fabricated engine results.

The candidate lab uses actual exported candidates. For merchant 082, the comparison
shows a $31,300 structure just below the review coverage boundary and a $31,200
structure at that boundary. The selected alternative remains review-required because the
capacity and human-judgment gates still matter. A separate lower-amount candidate
illustrates why passing capacity cannot override the minimum-amount rule.

Final authority is explained through the actual binding drivers. The case studies
distinguish merchant 738’s missing required evidence, merchant 082’s capacity and
confidence-related review, and merchant 098’s governed stress-floor reselection. The
deck does not equate the most attractive standalone return with the final permissible
choice.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P037-S01 -->

Monitoring is no longer reduced to a seasoning timeline. The five monitoring families
each have their inputs, eligibility, thresholds, and operational meaning explained.
Merchant 749’s actual day-level sequence then shows why its pace ratio can improve
while an unpaid horizon overrun keeps the overall status severe.

Intervention and reconciliation expose the implemented mechanics. The deck
identifies what the retained routing branch actually uses, separates recommendation from
setup and execution permission, and works through scheduled payments, a return, a retry,
net processing, and the remaining receivable.

Those examples and their exact source identities are documented in the selected case-
evidence companion.

What has been checked—and what remains

All 22 slides were constructed with native PowerPoint text and geometry, and an initial
complete render was inspected page by page. That inspection led to the final editorial
corrections now in the PowerPoint: clearer calculation assumptions, explicit definitions,

Page 14 is the supporting exhibit. Its heatmap compares Baseline outcomes along the
rows with Recession Energy outcomes along the columns. The diagonal represents
unchanged categories; the off-diagonal cells represent changes. Its largest changes
include 88 counteroffer-review paths becoming evidence declines, 51 becoming no-
feasible-structure declines, and 47 approve-as-requested paths becoming evidence
declines.

I independently reread the retained final-decision export, confirmed 750 complete
scenario pairs, and reconciled all 49 cells of the original transition matrix.

What it does—and does not—mean

This is not a comparison between software releases, a strategy-profile change, ora
chronological deterioration in real merchants. It compares two alternative scenarios within
the same retained source.

It also does not mean “236 approvals were lost.” Moving from approve as requested to
counteroffer authorized counts as a category change, even though both outcomes are
authorized. Conversely, different selected terms with the same final decision category do
not count toward this metric.

The population: 750 synthetic merchants, evaluated under two alternative scenarios.

The calculations: source-derived records supply the analytical figures and metrics—not
measurements estimated from screenshots.

Power BI experience: 32 Command Center, 32 Cohort Lifecycle, 28 Application Journey,
and 2 Servicing views. The underlying counts remain unchanged.

I also explained the Power BI acceptance references in plain language while preserving

authorization is not live-publication authorization. Those distinctions were already
present in the original page but were buried in internal shorthand.

### 0067 · assistant · timestamp unknown

<!-- BEGIN_RECORD P4-P055-S01 caa52cd26e1c9d55b85964d0132b57fb1da9bebc21f9ed45cf9727b31ed1be90 -->
What the comparison actually shows

The current pitch is not simply less attractive than its predecessors

It has substantial advantages. Its opening hierarchy is clearer, its individual messages are
easier to identify, and its counter-offer and authority examples are more concrete. It also
presents an actual Power BI view rather than relying entirely on conceptual diagrams of a
future learning platform. Those are improvements we should not undo.

Nor is the problem simply insufficient color or variety. The current deck already alternates
between navy, teal, and light pages. Adding more dramatic backgrounds would not
solve its central weakness.

The predecessors communicate a more distinctive business system

Their strongest diagrams do something beyond arranging information:

The merchant-centered radial maps show that acquisition, cash flow, capacity,
economics, risk, and operations are interconnected. The merchant journeys convey
continuity. The layered strategy engines show transformation and handoffs. The
roadmaps and endings explicitly connect capabilities to business purpose. These
strengths are particularly apparent on pages 3-6 of the eight-page edition and pages 3, 4,
6, 7,9, and 10 of the ten-page v2.

By contrast, the current deck repeatedly uses smallicons along a horizontal line. That is
clear, but often generic. Pages 3, 6, and 10 use closely related visual constructions for
three different narrative jobs.

The simplification removed some meaningful relationships—not just clutter. That is
the principal finding.

See the clearest example: current page 3 versus heritage v2 page 3
But the solution is not to restore the older density

The ten-page heritage edition has repeated status panels, large technology/governance
footers, numerous smallicons, and dense explanatory copy. Its system identity is stronger,
but its first-pass reading hierarchy is weaker. The eight-page edition is more restrained,
although its broad light treatment and small text create their own limitations.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P055-S01 -->

### 0068 · assistant · timestamp unknown

My recommendation is therefore:

Recover the heritage’s meaningful visual relationships while keeping the current
pitch’s reading clarity and factual maturity.

I would also qualify my earlier emphasis on making the pitch “cinematic.” The strongest
supplied heritage is primarily illustrated and diagrammatic, not photographic. A

compelling executive pitch does not require cinematic imagery on every page. It requires

visually distinctive ideas that express the business proposition.

### 0069 · assistant · timestamp unknown
**Record:** `P4-P057-S01` · **Fidelity:** `OCR_DERIVED_NORMALIZED_TRANSCRIPTION_PAGE_IMAGE_REVIEWED`
**Source:** P4 pages 57 · **Literal time label:** none

<!-- BEGIN_RECORD P4-P057-S01 5ff64aa5f2e39e0e5272e480d58945fbfde6eb15511a3ab241e87b536bc4e464 -->
1. Cover — REFINE

What works: This is the strongest cover of the three for title hierarchy and immediate
readability. The dark background, gold emphasis, and merchant illustration create an
identifiable opening. | would not discard that composition.

What limits it: The isolated storefront, floating payment card, and small chart
communicate commerce more readily than the transformation from a financing request
into portfolio intelligence. The three prominent counts establish implementation scope,
but do not themselves explain why the capability matters.

Recommended treatment: Keep the title and broad composition, but evolve the central
illustration into a purposeful merchant-request-to-portfolio visual. Make the business
receiving an advance—not payment processing—the unmistakable subject. Recover the
older covers’ explicit enterprise breadth without bringing back their status dashboard.

The cover needs a more meaningful hero illustration, not more material.

2. Sales, cash flow, and capacity — REFINE

What works: The three-part contrast is immediately understandable. It establishes an
important distinction without equations or excessive explanation.

What limits it: The smallicons and large empty intervals make the page feel closer to an
introductory lesson than a persuasive business-problem statement. Its “Launch with
Discipline” section label also fits the actual content less well than its main headline does.

Recommended treatment: Preserve the contrast, but make the interaction between
incoming sales, existing commitments, and requested financing visually stronger. Then
give the buyer one clear implication: the proposed terms must be evaluated against what
the business can support, not merely what it sells.

The eight-page predecessor’s second page has a useful quality here: it ends on a decisive
principle rather than merely completing a diagram. Borrow that decisiveness—not its six-
stage launch sequence, which serves a different topic.

3. Connected platform — RECONCEIVE

This is the clearest case where the heritage has a stronger visual idea.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P057-S01 -->

### 0070 · assistant · timestamp unknown
**Record:** `P4-P058-S01` · **Fidelity:** `OCR_DERIVED_NORMALIZED_TRANSCRIPTION_PAGE_IMAGE_REVIEWED`
**Source:** P4 pages 58 · **Literal time label:** none

<!-- BEGIN_RECORD P4-P058-S01 f708dd3f74c36ec58994410365d00ee58d138299399f967bc87b22b70f064f22 -->
The current four-circle sequence is clean, but it could describe many financial platforms. It
tells us to acquire, decide, operate, and learn without showing enough of the relationships
that make MSBF distinctive. “Acquire” is also paired primarily with merchant assessment
rather than a visible acquisition/attribution role.

Both predecessors’ Beyond Underwriting pages make the merchant-finance strategy
visibly multidimensional. Their radial structure is not just decorative: it communicates
interdependence.

Recommended treatment: Rebuild this around the merchant/request interaction anda
small number of connected business capabilities. Restore the supported acquisition -
attribution/economics context, then show how evidence connects to decisions, account
activity, and management insight.

This should be an executive systems diagram, not a reduced architecture poster and not
animplied autonomous learning loop.

4. Counter-offer strategy — REFINE

One of the strongest current pages. The headline is persuasive, the teal treatment
distinguishes it, and merchant 098 supplies a concrete source-based example. The
construction-versus-authority distinction is also explicit.

The opportunity is that the page currently shows a count and a result, but mostly
describes the search.

Recommended treatment: Retain the headline, case, scenario pairing, and guardrails.
Add a concise search-and-filter visual that makes permissible alternatives tangible. The
two scenario outcomes should remain side by side; the reader must not mistake them fora
chronological before-and-after.

Oneimportant boundary: 6,519 is the reportable-structure population across the
demonstration. It must not appear to be the number of alternatives tested for merchant
098 alone. My earlier suggested line “6,519 structures searched. One governed decision.”
would therefore be unsuitable without a much more explicit scope.

This page needs a stronger explanatory image—not replacement content.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P058-S01 -->

### 0071 · assistant · timestamp unknown
**Record:** `P4-P059-S01` · **Fidelity:** `OCR_DERIVED_NORMALIZED_TRANSCRIPTION_PAGE_IMAGE_REVIEWED`
**Source:** P4 pages 59 · **Literal time label:** none

<!-- BEGIN_RECORD P4-P059-S01 1931b85e215f0c3952433a46433ec683623ea00ca670fc0f4e89d8f843bbdf8f -->
5. Supportability versus authority — KEEP

This page already earns its place in a flagship pitch.

The mathematical decomposition, restrained proportional bar, selected-alternative case,
and outcome vocabulary reinforce one another. The page conveys that broader structural
opportunity does not automatically become approval volume. It gives the reader evidence
of mature credit judgment rather than merely asserting that judgment exists.

The accepted masterclass now explains the detailed candidate mechanics. The pitch
correctly extracts the executive conclusion instead of reproducing that lesson.

I would keep the composition and information balance. A later shared typography
adjustment may be appropriate, but there is no reason to add ornament merely to make
the revision look more substantial.

A strong deck needs quieter pages. This is a good one.

6. Funded lifecycle — RECONCEIVE

The headline and lifecycle-coverage measure are valuable. The weakness is the six-icon
line: itresembles page 3, while the endpoint dot display requires study before its meaning
becomes apparent. The diagram names operations more than it reveals their connection.

The heritage merchant journeys provide stronger visual continuity. The lighter eight-page
treatment uses an illustrated path; the ten-page treatment explicitly keeps the merchant
connected to the stages. Neither should return at its original density.

Recommended treatment: Use an illustrated account journey with a few meaningful
evidence and permission handoffs. Make alternative end states visible rather than
presenting intervention as something every account inevitably experiences.

Keep the existing coverage and endpoint facts. Change the central visual from “these
stages exist” to “this is how an accountable financing relationship is followed
through.”

7. Power BI reveal — REFINE

The actual product evidence is a major advantage over the predecessors. It should
stay.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P059-S01 -->

### 0072 · assistant · timestamp unknown
**Record:** `P4-P060-S01` · **Fidelity:** `OCR_DERIVED_NORMALIZED_TRANSCRIPTION_PAGE_IMAGE_REVIEWED`
**Source:** P4 pages 60 · **Literal time label:** none

<!-- BEGIN_RECORD P4-P060-S01 4b8462100dfac14aba9a17f07c3fadb6fe89734c5bb5199af7d61f3984ebbe48 -->
The current screenshot is large enough to recognize a functioning analytical surface, but
too small to comfortably study its details at an ordinary slide-view size. The left-hand P01-
P04 directory consumes substantial space without demonstrating one particular
management question in depth.

The older learning-system page is stronger at articulating the purpose of insight, but it
cannot compete with the current page’s actual analytical evidence.

Recommended treatment: Keep the screenshot and dark reveal. Give the evidence more
room, or pair a small full-view locator with a clearly labeled enlargement from the exact
screenshot. Highlight two executive questions and the visible evidence that addresses
them.

Do not create a fictional dashboard, alter displayed values, or reopen Power BI. Improve
the presentation of the evidence, not the underlying evidence.

8. Scenario proof — REFINE

The paired scenarios, explicit denominators, and modeled-contribution qualification are
strengths. The current cards communicate values clearly, but they do not make the
relationship as immediate as the best cohort exhibits.

There is also a specific information-design issue: the 59 > 130 strip introduces a software
predecessor/successor comparison on a page otherwise devoted to Baseline versus
Recession Energy. The labelis present, but the audience must switch comparison frames
at the bottom.

Recommended treatment: Keep the two-scenario exhibit and give it one common-scale
visual—authorization or funding—with a concise interpretation. Move the release-
comparison strip into supporting material, or separate it unmistakably.

The cohort report’s strength is its relationship-plus-explanation pairing, not simply its
number of metrics. That is what the pitch should borrow.

I would not restore the heritage Proof Before Scale page’s dense validation-count
presentation. The current page is much more business-facing.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P060-S01 -->

### 0073 · assistant · timestamp unknown
**Record:** `P4-P061-S01` · **Fidelity:** `OCR_DERIVED_NORMALIZED_TRANSCRIPTION_PAGE_IMAGE_REVIEWED`
**Source:** P4 pages 61 · **Literal time label:** none

<!-- BEGIN_RECORD P4-P061-S01 26512353821a936e6dfe525a9b88578329dca52fe5a7944b0932e528dab70472 -->
9. Roadmap — RECONCEIVE

The current delivery/development/conceptual separation is correct and should remain.
But the main visualis three ascending text cards, with module labels and status occupying
much of the explanatory space. It makes the program’s position clearer than its business
ambition.

The heritage roadmap does something valuable: it gives each horizon a purpose and
objective, not just a stage label. Its details are too dense and some claims belong to
historical or conceptual scope, but the capability-to-purpose relationship is stronger.

Recommended treatment: Preserve the three state boundaries while making the graphic
capability-led. For each horizon, identify the business question that richer capability would
help answer.

This is not permission to add promises, dates, or unbuilt functionality. Itis a change from
status reporting to a clearly bounded strategic proposition.

10. Closing proposition — RECONCEIVE

The current language is sensible and the invitation is restrained. Visually, however, the
ending returns to another line of circles and arrows. It repeats connectedness rather than
resolving the promise of the opening. Your name remains confined to the small footer.

Both older endings connect the proposition more explicitly to business value. The ten-page
version is particularly forceful, although its impact claims cannot simply be reused as
demonstrated outcomes.

The accepted masterclass also now has a more conclusive finish within its own teaching
role: it brings its acts together and leaves the learner with a standard for explanation.

Recommended treatment: Replace the repeated process row with a distinctive synthesis
of the opening merchant/request and the accountable portfolio it becomes part of.
Connect a small number of demonstrated capabilities to organizational questions. Make
Andrew R. Goad a readable, restrained signature, followed by a focused invitation to
discuss a business problem.

The closing should say, through its composition: “You have now seen what this
approach can connect.” It should not feel like one more process slide.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P061-S01 -->

Meaningful relationships—not additional decoration

The revision follows the audit’s exact scope: five refinements, four reconstructed
compositions, and one preserved page.

The heritage’s merchant-centered relationships, illustrated journey, and capability-to-
purpose roadmap informed the new diagrams. Their historical status panels, dense
technical footers, and broader capability claims did not carry forward.

[IMAGE/INTERFACE EVENT: placeholder or control; image content is not visible in this PDF]

Pages 1-3: make the business system visible

The opening now connects an advance request to portfolio intelligence. The storefront
remains a recognizable merchant-business illustration, but the floating payment-card
emphasis is gone. Request, structure, authority, and the funded path give the illustration a
specific financing meaning.

Page 2 retains Sales # Cash flow # Capacity, while making existing commitments and
requested terms more visible. Its conclusion addresses the buyer directly: evaluate terms
against merchant capacity—not sales volume alone.

Page 3 replaces the generic four-circle sequence with the merchant-centered system
map shown above. Acquisition context, merchant evidence, capacity/resilience, and
risk/economics converge on the merchant/request decision. The authorized, funded path
continues into account operations and source-grounded insight.

View the revised opening
Pages 4-5: show the search, preserve the judgment

Page 4 keeps the counter-offer headline and merchant 098’s matched-scenario
comparison. Acompact diagram now shows term construction > credit/economics
testing > final authority, with M2.2 and M2.3 visibly separated. The 6,519 reportable
structures remain labeled as demonstration-wide—not as one merchant’s search
population.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P064-S01 -->

PDF rendering is pixel-identical at the tested comparison scale. The quiet authority spread
remains the counterweight to the more illustrated pages around it.

View the revised structure-search page

Pages 6-8: connect the account story to actual evidence

Page 6 is now an account journey with branches, rather than another horizontal list of
stages. Afunded account retains its identity and evidence; monitoring leads into routing
and permission boundaries; the reconciled end states remain distinct. The design does not
imply that every account passes through a mandatory intervention ladder.

Page 7 presents two management questions beside enlarged evidence from the

small generic icons, repeated rounded containers, thin connectors, and a large
competing case card.

space or improve the overall composition enough.

I would withdraw the claim that R5 page 4 is a successful visual refinement. The
factual additions are useful; their presentation is not yet right.

### 0084 · assistant · timestamp unknown

R5 instead retains much of the newer dark-background, rounded-card, small-icon
vocabulary. So we borrowed a radial arrangement and a branching journey, but we
continued drawing them in a visual language you were already questioning.

That is the central execution miss.

4. The overall scheme still feels more assembled than
art-directed

Looking across the full sequence, | see a repeated pattern: large headline, short
explanation, a diagram assembled from cards and icons, then a concluding strip or
caption. The dark/light alternation provides variation, but the underlying construction
remains quite similar. Pages 1, 3, 4, 6, 9, and 10 all rely substantially on these small
diagram components.

Three things are weakening the finish.

First, decorative framing sometimes competes with the information. The diagonal
background division and lower curved lines recur, but do not explain the merchant-finance
proposition. They are not automatically wrong; they simply are not doing enough to justify
their prominence.

Second, the illustration styles are not fully unified. The isometric storefront, thin outline
icons, stacked document cards, oval hub, and dashboard -window illustration do not feel
equally resolved. They share colors, but shared colors alone do not produce a convincing
design system.

Third, too much of the sophistication remains in the sentences rather than the central
visual. “Accountable,” “governed,” “connected,” and “source-certified” describe real
qualities of the work. But the composition needs to make those qualities evident—not
repeatedly ask the reader to accept the adjectives.

That is why simply removing the oval and adjusting page 4 would not fully resolve your
discomfort with the deck.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P070-S01 -->

My assessment of the finished pitch

My overall editorial grade is A- for its intended role as an executive/client introduction
and hiring-portfolio presentation. That is a qualitative assessment, not a prediction of
hiring or commercial results.

The strongest improvement is that the visual presentation and the underlying argument
now reinforce each other. The opening establishes the breadth of the system. The
merchant-centered map shows why the relationships matter. The structure-search and
authority pages demonstrate judgment through concrete distinctions and cases. The
lifecycle page carries that discipline into operations, and the Power BI page now provides
authentic evidence rather than a visual approximation.

The conclusion also gives the presentation a clear destination: structure the opportunity,
govern the decision, and explain the portfolio, followed by a business question anda
readable author signature. It no longer ends as though another technical exhibit should
follow.

I would retain this version rather than continue broad aesthetic revisions. Additional
stylistic experiments are not necessary to clear this review and would risk disturbing work
that now holds together.

One observation, not a correction request
The full P01 screenshot on Page 7 is necessarily a small locator. Its smallest interface text

is better read in the original screenshot; the upper exhibits provide the focused evidence
for this page. That is consistent with the layout’s purpose and does not block signoff.

1. The overall story is now compelling

The strongest shared narrative is:

Understand the merchant. Test the requested structure. Find permissible

alternatives. Preserve decision authority. Follow the funded account. Explain the

portfolio.

That story runs from the merchant/request framing in the masterclass through the

architecture’s stage ownership and into the cohort’s application-to-servicing evidence. It

does not reduce merchant finance to either a score or an approval/decline result.

From a hiring perspective, the package’s strongest implication is not simply “Andrew

understands credit analytics.” It is:

“Andrew canconnect a business problem to data, decision logic, operating controls,

analytical evidence, and an explanation other people can use.”

Thatis an editorial assessment, not a guarantee of how a particular employer will respond.
Nevertheless, itis a much more substantial proposition than presenting an isolated
dashboard, model, or architecture diagram.

“Same world. Different camera” is working

The families now share an identity without requiring identical layouts:

The pitch persuades. It introduces the business proposition and demonstrates judgment
through selected evidence.

The architecture explains ownership and dependencies. It lets a technical reviewer
inspect how the system is organized.

The masterclass teaches causality. It explains why inputs, parameters, thresholds, and
authority conditions change the answer.

The cohort report examines results. It shows how those mechanisms manifest across
the synthetic population.

The posters should orient the reader. Their job is a compact, illustrated explanation—not
a substitute for the teaching deck or architecture.

The package’s weakness is therefore not a missing story. It is that the two quick-reference
explainers currently depart from the story’s factual meaning at several important points.

2. Client pitch — PASS

What works

The frozen ten-page pitch now has a professional executive-brief identity. Its light editorial
treatment, serif hierarchy, restrained illustrations, concrete merchant examples, and
closing signature feel coherent.

The strongest sequence is pages 4-5. Page 4 establishes why searching permissible
structures matters. Page 5 then prevents the reader from equating structural opportunity
with permission to fund. The merchant cases make that distinction tangible rather than
relying on a generic claim about governance.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P139-S01 -->

How it should be presented

This is a 53-page analytical read-ahead, not a 53-page LinkedIn pitch. Its length is
justified by its role, provided readers have clear entry points. The executive overview,
merchant cases, management implications, and facts sheet offer those entry points
without requiring a new shortened edition as a prerequisite.

One publication-status observation needs handling: page 48 still includes “Complete the
separate final cohort-report review.” That task was completed after the report’s dated
content was written. This is stale workflow prose, not an analytical defect. Preserve the
accepted edition and state its current acceptance/publication status in the gallery or
release record; do not silently rewrite it during packaging.

The private source/QA/reproducibility carrier must remain separate from the two
registered public report files. Also retain the distinction between R17’s Power BI
acceptance and the separate cohort-report approval.

5. Architecture trio — three individual
PASS decisions

Enterprise architecture

This is a strong engineering entry point. The seven functional bands, control plane, stage
ownership, consumption boundaries, counter-offer strategy, and horizon rail
communicate an integrated system rather than a collection of technologies.

The current/development/conceptual distinctions are visible, while the main map
preserves the flow into governed decisions, account operations, and certified
consumption. Keep it detailed. Its purpose is not to duplicate the pitch cover.

Module 1 lineage

This now demonstrates real evidence architecture. The input > logic > output/grain > role
treatment makes the thirteen core M1.2-M1.14 stages meaningful. The additional M1.15
application contract, M1.16 acquisition companion, and M1.17 G2 assurance remain
distinct rather than being collapsed into a generic “validation” box.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P142-S01 -->

This artifact contributes something particularly valuable to the hiring story: it shows

Module 2 lineage

The twelve-stage view connects strategy and operating accountability. It distinguishes
candidate construction/testing from final authority; funding from operational setup;
recommendations from execution; and account reconciliation from portfolio
consumption. Its case and endpoint exhibits keep those distinctions connected to
business meaning.

No additional content or design correction is requested for any of the three.

The appropriate publication response to their density is full-resolution/vector access
and aclear preview, not smaller type, forced aspect-ratio uniformity, or removal of the
detail that makes them useful.

6. How the Governed Decision Engine
Works — retain the design; hold factual
clearance

The poster’s circuit framing, shield, rich icons, five numbered panels, downward
progression, and strong close still work. It is visually one of the package’s more
immediately recognizable pieces.

However, three areas need correction before itis presented as an explanation of the
current implementation.

The gate and authority explanation is too broad
The poster says “If a hard gate fails > stop or review.” Its truth-table concept sends a

supportable structure with failed gates to human assessment. That does not distinguish a

preserve those distinctions at a compact level.

seven-route family; retain it rather than rebuilding the taxonomy again.

The capability inventory extends beyond the reviewed implementation

The poster includes renewal/step-down search, generic rate/fee/blend wording, covenant
controls, and device/velocity examples. Those labels are not established as delivered

dimensions—amount, remittance, payback multiple, and horizon—with economics asa
required test. Retain the supported 40%/60% and 30/60/90-day guardrails.

“Final offer,” renewalterms, and a “reproducible contract” described as templated terms
imply more than the versioned decision/data-consumption outputs established
elsewhere. Not every governed outcome produces an authorized offer.

versioned data/consumption contract. Preserve the reason-code, rationale, and
evidence-trail concepts.

These changes belong inside the existing panels. They do not justify discarding the
artwork.

### 0172 · assistant · timestamp unknown
**Record:** `P4-P145-S01` · **Fidelity:** `OCR_DERIVED_NORMALIZED_TRANSCRIPTION_PAGE_IMAGE_REVIEWED`
**Source:** P4 pages 145 · **Literal time label:** none

<!-- BEGIN_RECORD P4-P145-S01 c56063434bad1df277fbbbfc62a142bfae4d7b6e795f0bf8beb2367bf794de68 -->
7. What Happens After the Decision —
retain the design; repair the relationships

This poster has a good visual foundation and an especially useful business -
question/evidence table. Its problems are concentrated in the meanings assigned to that
structure.

Monitoring families and statuses are mixed together

The poster labels Watch, Underperforming, Severe Shortfall, and Dormant/No Remittance
as operational alert families. The finalized teaching system distinguishes those statuses
from the five families: pace, no remittance, sales dormancy, liquidity, and horizon.

It also describes horizon pressure as less time remaining, whereas the retained source test
concerns an unpaid balance beyond the selected horizon.

Required treatment: separate conditions, families, and status. Keep the existing 21/30/3
strip explicitly scoped to pace.

The intervention staircase teaches the wrong operating relationship

The staircase suggests progression through outreach, adjustment, servicing, and closure.
Its closure card includes an account that “naturally reaches horizon.”

That directly conflicts with the finalized masterclass, cohort, and corrected pitch.
Reaching the horizon does not extinguish a receivable. Recommendation does not
establish setup or processing permission. Aheld route does not become an executed
action. The accepted cohort demonstrates these distinct states explicitly.

Required treatment: retain the icon vocabulary but replace the local staircase with
alternative routes and permission boundaries. The corrected pitch’s Page 6 already
provides a reviewed relationship model.

Certification is shown after Power BI

The final panel’s arrows place Power BI views before G3-v2 certification. The architecture
has the opposite dependency: source outputs are certified, then consumed in Power BI.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P145-S01 -->

Required treatment: show reconciled account/portfolio evidence and source analysis >
G3-v2 source certification > Power BI consumption.

Some captions imply an operating product rather than a synthetic
demonstration

“Live” accounts, verified legal agreements/disclosures, real-time receivable estimates,
and a broad severity/persistence/impact prioritization queue read as delivered operational
capabilities. The retained source material supports a narrower, explicitly synthetic funding
and servicing demonstration with stated routing rules and source-owned balances.

Both posters also need the concise synthetic / non-production / no real funds scope
note carried by the other families.

The same discipline that corrected the pitch should now finish these two posters:
preserve the look; correct the assertions.

8. Cross-artifact consistency is strong
elsewhere

The recurring cases and quantities are doing useful work rather than becoming repetitive
decoration.

Merchant 098 connects an executive counteroffer example to underlying capacity
calculations and matched-scenario selection. Merchant 082 connects the selected-but-
review-required proposition to its binding gates. Merchant 738 explains why missing
evidence cannot be repaired merely by changing terms. Merchant 749 connects
monitoring state to permitted servicing and the remaining receivable.

The main numerical meanings remain aligned across the six passing units: 750 merchants
versus 1,500 scenario paths; 6,519 structures versus approvals; 300/80 supportable
paths versus 103/27 authorized paths; 130 funded scenario paths and 15,600 account-
days; and zero unresolved exceptions versus a nonzero remaining balance. The
cohort’s definitions and the architecture’s endpoint exhibits preserve those distinctions.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P146-S01 -->

### 0174 · assistant · timestamp unknown
**Record:** `P4-P147-S01` · **Fidelity:** `OCR_DERIVED_NORMALIZED_TRANSCRIPTION_PAGE_IMAGE_REVIEWED`
**Source:** P4 pages 147 · **Literal time label:** none

<!-- BEGIN_RECORD P4-P147-S01 bc03c03f301ada11a43b0c9f8c36ebdbb4a363e6754332e49dc433580f85e818 -->
The problem with the posters is therefore particularly important: a reader should not
learn a nuanced distinction inthe masterclass and then encounter the opposite
implication in the quick-reference illustration.

9. Publication should organize the depth—
not homogenize it

Once the poster corrections are accepted, | recommend an audience-led gallery rather
than one compulsory combined report.

For a hiring manager or client, lead with the pitch, then offer the enterprise map and
selected cohort evidence. The corrected posters can provide a quick mechanism
overview; the masterclass and detailed lineage should be available for deeper inspection.

For technical reviewers, the architecture and code/evidence companions can be the entry
point. For learners, the masterclass should be discoverable directly.

“Architect it - Sell it - Teach it - Prove it” remains a useful organizing concept, but the
reader’s purpose should determine the order.

Publication preparation should preserve exact editions, give each artifact a clear purpose
and audience, provide full-size/accessibility options, and separate public deliverables
from private review/source carriers. The frozen pitch ZIP, for example, is not a blanket
instruction to publish every included support file.

There is also one packaging-only observation: the architecture ZIP currently mounted
here was not readable as a standard ZIP, although its PDF, PNGs, and SVG/PPTX
companions were separately available and readable. This does not establish that the
originally delivered archive was corrupt. The final publication archive should be rebuilt or
recovered from identified files and verified during packaging; this does not revoke the
architecture artwork signoff.

Final decision
The package has achieved a strong visual identity and a persuasive, technically

grounded story. | would preserve the six passing artifacts without further broad
revisions.
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P147-S01 -->

structure conditions, judgment requirements, and advisories remain distinct. The stop

waive a non-remediable prohibition. That matches the accepted teaching explanation
rather than compressing every failure into “stop or review.”

Structure support and final authority are now clearly separated. The revised parameter
panel distinguishes the remittance share, advance amount, payback multiple, and
horizon. The economic hurdle remains a required test, and the 40% minimum / 60%
maximum reduction boundaries are intact. M2.2 constructs and tests; M2.3 resolves final

descriptions.

record &terms” and “Versioned data contract” no longer imply that every path produces

synthetic scope note are visible.

The factual repair has not stripped away the poster’s identity. The naw circuit frame,
shield, illustrated cards, numbered progression, and gold close still provide the premium
vertical-poster treatment you wanted.

preserves the important distinctions. It gives the reader a clear organizing idea without
trying to reproduce the entire executable decision process.

The operating relationships are now correct

staircase.

Monitoring families and statuses are separated. The five overlapping families are
distinct from the four displayed non-current statuses. The 21/30/3 treatment is explicitly
limited to pace, and horizon is described as an unpaid balance beyond the selected
horizon—not merely less time remaining. These distinctions agree with the accepted
monitoring lessons.

Recommendation no longer implies execution. Panel 3 now shows the observed
population appropriately:

¢ 126 paid-off paths proceed to a certified paid-off state without implying a new
servicing action.

e Three outreach-review paths pass through setup and processing permissions and
remain held.

e Onetemporary-review path passes through those permissions before permitted
servicing and account-state certification.

The scope caption ties these counts to the 130 funded scenario paths across two
alternatives. | read this as the observed population summary—not as a claim that every
future outreach recommendation must be held or every temporary-review
recommendation must be executed. That is consistent with the retained source, which

Yes — that is a different question, and the answer is that the platform was explicitly
being engineered so a user could define and run new synthetic campaigns of their
own.

My previous answer conflated two separate concepts:

Reproducing the exact accepted R26 campaign from an empty database
versus

Using the MSBF framework to configure and execute a new campaign.
Those are not the same requirement.

The Campaign Scale workstream was specifically designed around a governed parameter
layer so campaign identity, population, seeds, scenarios, strategies, dates, expected row-
count formulas, checkpoints, evidence retention, and restart policy could be changed
without rewriting the accepted M1/M2 business logic. It explicitly called for changed
scale to propagate without SQL edits.

And the intended operating model was very clear:
<!-- END_RECORD P4-P178-S02 -->

### 0213 · assistant · timestamp unknown
**Record:** `P4-P179-S01` · **Fidelity:** `OCR_DERIVED_NORMALIZED_TRANSCRIPTION_PAGE_IMAGE_REVIEWED`

**Editorial/source-status annotations:** Historical campaign-use explanation; not qualified current operating instructions. Historical campaign instructions are not verified operating instructions. <!-- BEGIN_RECORD P4-P179-S01 478efaa64d8b5e2d7ee5c3c106d1b83ef5f56a01175cf68cb49525b082e2f2af -->
parameterize the campaign context > orchestrate with PowerShell and psql > fail closed >
checkpoint/resume > retain evidence > archive the database

with the goal of turning hundreds of governed program positions into essentially one
human campaign command.

So conceptually, yes: a user should be able to create a different campaign.

What “new campaign” means in this system
A user should be able to supply a new campaign profile such as:

¢ anew campaign_id/campaign_version;

e adifferent merchant population size;

e different deterministic seeds;

e chosen scenarios;

e chosen strategy configuration;

e application/funding/monitoring/as-of dates;

e campaign-specific run IDs;

e governed thresholds and product-policy settings;
e evidence/export and retention settings.

That profile then drives the accepted modular pipeline rather than requiring the user to edit
dozens or hundreds of downstream SQL programs. The campaign design explicitly
required 750, 2,500 and 25,000 profiles, but also required parameterization rather than
replacing one hard-coded scale with another.

Module 2 itself was also written with this idea in mind. Its strategy-control section explains
how a new run changes fields such as strategy_name, strategy _run_id, Module 1
input, scenario/population identity, risk/PTI/DTI thresholds, archive behavior, and
campaign registry settings. Product-level policy is changed through the governed product-
policy parameters rather than downstream CASE logic.

So your expectation is absolutely aligned with the architecture.

The important caveat

What! cannot yet say from the evidence we have reviewed is:

The publication requirement | think you are actually articulating

For a serious GitHub user, the repository should support something like:

1. Install PostgreSQL / prerequisites
2. Create a disposable campaign database
3. Copy or create a campaign profile
4. Set:
population
scenarios
seed
strategy
dates
policy parameters
5. Run one campaign command
6. System executes the governed M1/M2 sequence
7. Fail closed if a checkpoint fails
8. Produce evidence + output package
9. Optionally point Power BI at the resulting campaign outputs

Thatis a very different user experience from:
“Download my accepted R26 checkpoint and replay my exact results.”

And for the portfolio you are trying to publish, | agree the first experience is the more
important product capability.

Publication presentation and run-guide direction

The owner confirmed that the repository must represent the work effectively to hiring
managers and technical users—not merely satisfy packaging checks.

The intended documentation preserves individual M#.# code and documentation, provides
a clear current-use guide grounded in the actual available source and prerequisites, and
retains the detailed reproducibility runbook for reviewers and maintainers. Anew
generalized campaign runner is deferred to subsequent campaign-scale work.

The history must also retain the correction to the assistant’s earlier broad assertion about
running all approximately 230 programs in sequence: the historical catalog alone does
not establish a verified current R26 end-to-end procedure. That earlier statement must
not become operating guidance simply because it appears in the transcript.

Fidelity and duplicate handling

There are two important limits to preserve.

First, these are screenshot-based PDFs, not native structured chat exports. Both
arrived without parsed text. They can support page-referenced transcription or clearly
labeled summaries, but their presence does not establish recovered message IDs, exact
timestamps, or text hidden by a screenshot’s horizontal clipping. The original PDFs should
remain unchanged in private source retention.

Second, this long Part 4 conversation is not entirely available verbatim in the current
